ParlaMint-AT_2019-04-25-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00072_d7e3176	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Minister! For us farmers, resources from the common agricultural policy are an important component of income and we also increase the value added in our farms through climate protection measures and our ecological benefits. My question is: 111/M “What benchmarks are important for Austria in the upcoming CAP?”
ParlaMint-AT_2019-04-25-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00072_d7e3201	Please, Mrs. Federal Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2019-04-25-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00072_d7e3216	Thank you very much, honourable Member, for the question. – In fact, the Common European Agricultural Policy is a very important pillar of our functioning food system in Austria and Europe. We are again facing the challenge of reforming the common agricultural policy at European level. It is the only common policy area we find in this way. For us, the security of supply of European citizens and, of course, the Austrians are the top priority, in particular, in terms of quality production and environmental and climate protection. Our farmers also play an important part in this. For us as the Federal Government, of course, the financial structure of the common agricultural policy is also crucial in this area. According to the Commission proposal, there are also massive cuts in rural development. This is really unacceptable for us, and to this extent, because rural development in particular ensures that the environmental and agricultural services are provided by farmers. This includes, for example, our compensation for the mountain area, support for organic farming and investment support. This is really a very central point for us. We want to put farmers' family farms in the focus of the common agricultural policy. We want to get away from the system that the quantity, that is, the quantity, is promoted to a real quality system, which only our rural family farms ensure. We really want to see a paradigm change in the way that the payments of the common agricultural policy in the future are really available to our rural family farms. The third point, which is very important to us, is the agricultural environment programmes. These are also with a life vein of our rural structure in Austria, it does many things. This will focus more on climate protection, particularly in the future.

From Vorarlberg to Burgenland, there is an extremely wide variety, and we also want to anchor it more closely in the culinary network, to lower it with a system of origin and quality, thus giving the rural initiatives the opportunity to market themselves better and also to strengthen the networking with tourism. We want to ensure that specifically through the network of culinarys.

It has created the greatest possible harmonisation and harmonisation, a smallest common denominator. By the end of the year, the countries must adopt their respective implementing laws. As an disabled speaker, of course, I am particularly concerned with those points that concern people with disabilities. The government is also concerned that people such as single-parents or people with disabilities are now being improved in the new Basic Social Assistance Act. Moreover, regulations for people with disabilities are an absolute novum as far as the Social Assistance Act is concerned, because in the earlier 15a agreements there has been no regulations in this respect at all. It has also been substantially improved in the field of disability compared with the first draft of the law and has been very well addressed to the opinions of the stakeholders. Now on the relevant points that concern people with disabilities: In the future, people with disabilities will receive a minimum guarantee supplement of 18 percent of the net compensation allowance rate. This corresponds to around 160 euros per month. A must determination was made from the original canning determination. This means that countries are obliged to pay this additional benefit. In addition, it is not appropriate for countries to take account of special needs through other or additional regulations. Of course, everything that has so far been in the form of additional benefits may continue to be granted by the countries. It is also possible to grant additional benefits in cases of hardness. The Principles Act also makes it clear that the increased family allowance, the care allowance or further care and disability benefits are completely unaffected. Countries are also given the opportunity to establish their own needs for people with disabilities in order to meet the various forms of housing and living. Furthermore, people with disabilities can be excluded from the budget cover. They are supposed to get their performance unabated. For example, what caused a great deal of concern to deaf people was a possible demonstration of language competence. This too is clarified and clearly regulated in the law. From the provision of language skills All people with disabilities are excluded from successful language learning, such as people with complex communication disorders. The amendment concerning donations has already been addressed more precisely. These are also changes that can be seen very positively. I am particularly pleased that these new regulations are designed to counter the increased risk of poverty for people with disabilities. Thank you.

This has been demonstrated in particular by the previous Presidency in the Council of the European Union. Austria is also the location of many international organisations. Among other things, the United Nations here in Vienna is one of its four seats, and recently the expansion of important international organisations at the Vienna site has also been announced. Austria will, of course, continue to be an important bridge builder at European and international level. I believe that we are all therefore well advised not to adapt our good image abroad. This has already had a very bad effect on the Austrian population in the past.

ParlaMint-AT_2019-04-25-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00072_d7e19760	Madam President. Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! 2010 was declared by the United Nations as the year of biodiversity, biodiversity. In October 2010, the UN Convention on Biodiversity was held in Nagoya, Japan. I was allowed to participate as an Environment Minister at the time. It was a time when there was a great deal of criticism of the international, especially at the UN conferences. In 2009, a few months before that, the UN Climate Change Conference took place in Copenhagen under enormous expectations. The US had a new head of government with Obama and there was the prospect that the US would do more for climate protection. All the more so, the disappointment that the US did not do anything and that new players were on the world stage with China, Brazil and India, who said, had to be negotiated with them in order to reach a global climate agreement. The Copenhagen conference brought little output. At that time, the discussion was about the meaning of such UN conferences, because there is unanimity, that is, all 193 Member States must agree, and if only one state is against it, there is no agreement. The more positive it was then – although the scepticism was great – that Nagoya had a unanimous decision, namely an international commitment to a international treaty, namely the Nagoya Protocol, in which all the Contracting States committed themselves to the conservation of species and to the protection of biodiversity. The objectives of this international Convention on Biological Diversity are: firstly, to preserve biodiversity; secondly, to ensure access to genetic resources; and thirdly, to ensure a fair distribution, so that those who do not necessarily benefit from it benefit directly. A global strategy has been developed and adopted. The greatest success if you want to do so was the common goal to reduce the loss of biodiversity in the animal and plant world. Some have even spoken of stops, but this is really hopeless. Another point was the clear commitment of the Member States to include biodiversity, for example of plants as a value in the economy, so that environmental protection and economic development are no longer a contradiction. An important part of the protocol is access and benefit sharing, and the other is a strategic plan for biodiversity protection measures. Access and Benefit Sharing is about genetic resources. Plants, animals, microbial materials contain genetic resources. For example: In rainforests there are plants that can be used economically for medicinal products, cosmetics and the like. The deal of this pact was that there should be access to these genetic resources and not only the pharmaceutical companies deserve it, but also those people who live in the region concerned, for example indigenous peoples. The money should then also be used to enable economic development there. That was a great success. A similar debate was held yesterday: The copyright directive is, in fact, about the protection of property, and this is also about the protection of natural resources and, indeed, the possibility of economic exploitation. That was the one point. The other point was the strategic plan. Under the title ‘life in accordance with nature’, States around the world have committed themselves to creating more terrestrial protected areas, i.e. protected areas on land, and more marine protected areas, in the coastal waters, but also on high seas. In the course of the years, a lot has happened in the areas of land protection and coastal protection, but less in the high seas. That is, there is still a lot to be done. In conclusion: Austria has done something very important in the context of the EU Presidency and has pushed forward a ban on disposable plastic. This was an important contribution to reducing the pollution of the seas by microplastics, which there threatens biodiversity. It is also a question of defining a competent authority in this law. Thankfully, the BMNT serves as the responsible authority in Austria. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2019-04-25-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00072_d7e20036	Mrs. Köstinger has now spoken. - Please, Mrs. Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2019-04-25-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00072_d7e20057	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. High house! With the Federal Act for the Implementation of Obligations under the Nagoya Protocol, we take an important step towards the implementation of the international obligation already addressed several times. Austria has been a party to the Protocol since October 2018. The concrete implementation was still outstanding. With the present Federal Act, these points are now clearly regulated for Austria. The Federal Ministry of Sustainability and Tourism will take over the task of the competent authority. We can also draw on the expertise of the Federal Environment Agency, in particular in the field of controls. Where sanctions have to be imposed, the provisions of the Administrative Criminal Law or the General Administrative Procedure Act shall apply. In principle, it is necessary to distinguish between access to genetic resources in Austria and access to genetic resources by Austrian actors in other countries. Access in Austria remains free and is not additionally regulated, but provisions of the Nature Conservation Act, for example, apply. Access by Austrian actors, such as research institutes, collections, companies and the like in other countries which regulate access under the Nagoya Protocol shall be subject to: The provisions and conditions of the country of origin must be complied with. The competent authority in Austria must monitor this regularly and, in the event of infringements, also ensure any sanctions. I can assure you that we will make implementation, in particular this area of controls, as slim and resource-efficient as possible, so that users of genetic resources do not have a significant additional burden. As a next step, we will invite all relevant Austrian actors to a working group and we are pleased with proposals on how we can make concrete implementation efficient and, above all, practical. – Thank you very much.

It is therefore all the more important that more money be pumped into research and development in a country like Austria and that this technology is also exported to countries that have plenty of sun, wind or wave movements, such as the ocean. We can make a bridge with a reasonable policy in this area so that our economy has something to do with ensuring jobs. We can thus also protect other countries such as Turkey from mistakes. Turkey is now investing billions in a Russian technology to build nuclear power plants, even in an earthquake area. What would it mean if there was an accident there, an incident? – You are in Turkey in one and a half hours by plane today! The nuclear problem, therefore, is not something that concerns only certain national states or those countries which have no nuclear power plants and no nuclear waste storage sites like Austria, but their neighbouring countries. It's a global problem. We should get away from this thinking that everything that is not, so to speak, in our field of vision, not in Austria's territory or in that neighbouring country is not a matter of concern to us, but of global thinking. That is why it is important that we have a minister and a minister who is massively opposed to the expansion of nuclear power. Heartfelt Thank you for that! But, I think, we must lead the discussions very consistently and hard, because the atomic lobby is a very powerful lobby and is not so easily forced into the knee. Thank you.

In short: As a state museum, a Federal Museum is not there to complete works of art, and the Court of Auditors recommends that the Federal Minister or the Government, in particular with regard to permanent lending, should develop appropriate guidelines. A federal museum is an educational establishment and not a washing machine. That is, if the provenance, the origin of the images, the works of art is not clear, it is not the task of the museum to wash them cleanly. A Federal Museum is also not a throughputer for private works of art. This means that the value does not need to be increased by presentation, catalogue designations and so on. A federal museum is also not a thermal panel, where private works of art are very well preserved, archived, maintained, insured, restored. A Federal Museum is also not a centrifuge, with which forgerys can be distinguished at the expense of the generality of true works of art, with which private works of art can be distinguished from counterfeiting at the expense of the public. Two examples: The Batliner collection has already been mentioned. I repeat what I have said very often, here in plenary many years ago: The Batliner collection comes from a lawyer. That is, the Bundesmuseum is a kind of sales gallery, and we are, so to speak, those who finance all of this. He has no risk at all, we have the risk: We have the costs, we have the personnel to finance, and he has the profit. This is the effect of a permanent loan if there are no restrictive guidelines. ParlaMint-AT_2019-04-25-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00072_d7e22691	Madam President. Madam President of the Court of Auditors! Dear ladies and gentlemen! High house! Based on the Party Act in force since 2012, each political party must submit an accounting report to the Court of Auditors annually, and this report must include a list of the so-called participating companies. What are investment firms? – These are companies in which the political parties, their related organisations or political parties that own their own legal personality hold at least 5 percent direct shares or 10 percent indirect shares or voting rights. The Court of Auditors shall forward this list of undertakings to those undertakings subject to the control of the Court of Auditors and shall then be invited to communicate to the Court of Auditors, both in the overall and in detail, the transactions concluded with those undertakings. What is the aim of this party law? – The aim is to increase transparency with regard to the financing of all the political parties in Austria. The aim is also to make the business activities of political parties' participating parties transparent by public authorities or under their influence. The transparency of money flows is therefore decisive. In concrete terms, the report discussed: This is about the review of the reports submitted by the Ministry of Education – and it should be noted that this criticism is points are already to be found in test reports on other bodies' notifications. What has the Court of Auditors found? I'm allowed to get out of here a few days ago. In particular, it concerns the concept of legal conduct as defined in the law. According to the Court of Auditors, there are areas of interpretation which lead to different reports and which differ from the Court of Auditors' point of view. The definition of the concept of participation companies is also unclear, according to this report. This concludes the Court of Auditors' need for legislative action to clarify these terms. On the other hand, the Federal Chancellor's Office believes that the different interpretations in practice are not due to the legislative text. Anyway, ladies and gentlemen: However, since communities are also concerned, it is important to me as mayor to make clear wherever possible so that all the bodies examined can make correct and complete reports to the Court of Auditors in the sense of transparency. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2019-04-25-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00072_d7e23157	The President of the Court of Auditors has spoken. I'll give it to her.
ParlaMint-AT_2019-04-25-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00072_d7e23178	Mr President. High house! Three reports on reporting obligations in accordance with party law were now available for the debate on the example of the Ministry of Education, Art for Art Theaterservice GmbH and the follow-up review of the state studios of the ORF. The reporting obligations under the Party Act are the reporting obligations of the legal entities subject to the control of the Court of Auditors as regards legal transactions with participating parties of political parties and participating companies, in which parties have 5 percent directly or 10 percent indirectly shares. In this context, the Court of Auditors has already produced four reports: At different levels, namely at a ministry, at national level, in a community and in a society. It was a question of checking once again whether these reporting obligations have a say in how they will be met, because on the occasion of the report or the question we cannot examine how this corresponds to reality. In the example of the Ministry of Education, we have identified a discrepancy for reporting in the year, a discrepancy with regard to the reported and actually paid payments. One of the reasons we have seen was, in our opinion, an unclear regulation in the law. The Federal Chancellery is of a different opinion, but the fact is that there are different opinions, and this should be clarified. To sum up, the 2012 Party Act was strongly committed to the objective of transparency and to increasing transparency. These consultation obligations do not comply sufficiently with this, and in reality we cannot make conclusions on the nature and appropriateness of the legal transactions. That is why the flaws we have already shown are more often, and it is, of course, up to the National Council to improve these weaknesses and to comply with the principle of transparency. The second report concerns the subsidiary of the Bundestheater-Holding and three stage companies, Art for Art Theaterservice GmbH. We have reviewed these for the first time; in 1999 it was founded. It was already discussed that the real estate transactions were a criticism point, it was a volume of 45.79 million euros – in fact, they were done naturally – and the profits were distributed to the Bundestheater Group. The Art for Art therefore contributed significantly to the economic survival of the Bundestheater Group. On the one hand, we have found insufficient documentation and no public search for interests. On the other hand, we pointed out that it was a one-time action. In total, the other economic behaviour of this society was also examined. In particular, we have criticized two points: There are historically growing situations, pricing of the kind for art and personnel expenses. In the pricing there is a part where benefits did not cover the costs and another part where the benefits covered the costs. The Group's external orders offered non-cost-covering prices at 76 percent of the sales revenues of the decoration workshops. In this respect, we demand calculation principles and cost-effective calculation. On the staff effort: 11 percent of the staff costs are due to certain overpayments, rewards and individual allowances, and of course there must be criteria. There are also theatre holidays that do not have a positive impact on production costs and on the cost situation. Compliance was addressed – conflicts of interest with the head of the decoration workshop. As far as the ORF is concerned, this was a follow-up review of an audit from 2015. I would point out that there is also a demand procedure which is also publicly available on our homepage. Two points are open: that, as in our recommendations, the production costs of the consignments “Bundesland today” should be evaluated regularly. There are very different factors of influence, on the one hand the cost differences in production and on the other, the success indicators, these are the market share and the average range; and the topic of the technical directors, including this recommendation, is still open, so how one can exploit synergies. I would like to thank you for bringing my examiners to my attention, and I will point it out to them. - Thank you.

The green current for this hydrogen drive came from the back Zillertal. There, the Verbund AG generates a quarter of the Tyrolean electricity, and the Zillertalbahn would only need 1 percent of it. With the future hydrogen-powered railway as a backbone, we want to turn the most tourism-intensive valley into a CO2-neutral region – starting with the public up to the slopes. That would be the project, but what I am now experiencing, Minister, this has nothing to do with innovation. I have your support, including the support from Mrs. Köstinger, a warm thanks for it, and I feel good about you too, but at the official level, the old thinking of the Ministry of Transport presupposes: The liability is shifted back to the country of Tyrol, from the country of Tyrol or even to the Swiss train supplier. That's not how it can be done. [[Congratulations on MEPs of the ÖVP.]] The Republic or the Federal Ministers have signed all the treaties between Kyoto and Paris and Katowice, which is why, I think, the Republic and not the country of Tyrol is also obliged to go there. Unfortunately, the Zillertal cannot vote economically because of these favourable transport contracts which we must enter into. We need an accompaniment for these new trains, which are of course all prototypes. We have submitted the Hytrain project to the Climate and Energy Fund. In this project we would like to accompany the production of the drive line with the partners FEN-Systems in Innsbruck, the Technical University in Graz, Magna, OMV, Molinari Rail and so on. We would like to talk about which fuel cells, which hydrogen tanks are installed, how refuelling times must look like the hydrogen purity has to be. We also want to control air conditioning, energy management and all of this. This, of course, requires that we constantly intervene in deliveries and in liabilities with this scientific support. That's where officials think and it's said: That's not how it is! That's why we need you to help us. It will be an art to find a flexible way to allow innovation, new technology and control, also to ensure the know-how for Austria while maintaining the delivery obligation and liability of the producer company. That's a big challenge. We need a political decision to do this, Mr Federal Minister, because at the level of officials I am unfortunately no longer going on. – Thank you and congratulations on this novel.

ParlaMint-AT_2019-04-25-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00072_d7e25535	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! We have here in our Parliament seven standing subcommittees, two of which are subject to confidentiality and confidentiality protection. Infringements are punished with up to three years in prison. In order to review the measures to protect constitutional bodies and their ability to act, the Committee on Internal Affairs shall elect its Subcommittee. At the same time, the Subcommittee of the National Defence Committee is responsible for the control of the Defence Office and the Military News Office. Especially in the field of military intelligence, there are currently developments that require a long-awaited strengthening of parliamentary control. From 15.1. to 26.2., the Constitutional Law was reviewed, with the aim of amending, inter alia, the Military Power Act, with the aim of expanding the competences of the military intelligence services and of redesigning them in accordance with the Federal Office for Constitutional Protection and Combating Terrorism. In particular, the aim is to set up a new information procurement unit. The ==References====External links== This year. In a response to a request, however, it was also known that the complete re-examination of all organizational units can be possible. The former Federal Office is to be further developed towards a real intelligence service, but already in the last legislative period, the criticism has been voiced that the control of the two permanent subcommittees – for internal and national defence – is inefficient. The following points are to be considered and discussed: Can the Minister in a secret committee also call for national security to be at risk? How does it look with a right to access the file? How does it look with informants? Can you ask them? I would also propose a change of name for the two titles that are locked down would then be the control committee for internal and national defence the easier variant. Finally, the question raised by the Legal Protection Officer may be referred to Parliament for a settlement in order to ensure independence from the Minister, but also for the acquisition of all legal protection officials; for example, the judicial body would also have to be involved. Let us discuss this in the Committee on the Rules of Procedure. Let us start the discussion so that we can also change this law!
